Implementation details:
- Use Python list for points. For candidate evaluation, compute differences to each point; if any diff_count[d] >= max_diff, conflict. n up to maybe 5582, target size up to 120, candidate loop O(n*target) per epoch. 5582*120=670k, for 20 epochs 13M, Python loops maybe okay but could be slow. We can optimize by precomputing conflict candidates? For each point y, it forbids x where x±y has difference d with count already max? Actually if max_diff=1, add point x valid if for all y, diff d not already used. We can maintain a boolean allowed array length n, initially all 1. When add point y, for each currently valid? We can update allowed: new point x will be invalid if there exists y with |x-y| used. For each used diff d, mark x=y±d invalid if not already. But difference can become used multiple times? For T=1, when a diff count reaches limit, mark all positions y such that y±d candidate invalid? Actually if diff d is already used, no future point can have difference d to any existing point. So for each existing point y, mark y±d as forbidden. When a new diff d becomes used at adding point x, we need mark for all existing y? Wait condition: after set S, if diff d is used among S, then for any x not in S, x cannot be within distance d of any y in S (else would create duplicate diff). So we can maintain forbidden array: for each used diff d, for each y in S, forbid y+d and y-d. When adding new point x, before adding, all currently forbidden excluded. After adding, mark x itself removed from candidates. Also, for each existing diff d that now? Actually once d used, any future candidate cannot be at distance d from *any* existing point, including x. So after adding, for each new diff d, forbid x±d? No, for all y in S, forbid y±d. But if d was just used by pair (x,y1), then for every other old y, y+d and y-d may not be forbidden yet. But those positions if chosen would create duplicate diff with y. So need for each new diff d, loop over all points y and forbid y±d. That's O(k) per new diff, total O(k^3) maybe 120^3=1.7M, fine. For T>1, forbid only when count reaches max. Could maintain counts and allowed via conflict test on the fly simpler.

Simpler on-the-fly with vectorized NumPy:
- For current points pts array, candidate positions 0..n-1 not used. For each candidate, compute differences vector? def greedy_sidon(n, target_size, max_diff=1, seed=0, start_points=None, epochs=200):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    used=np.zeros(n,bool); counts=np.zeros(n,int8) maybe max_diff small int
    pts=[]
    if start_points: add valid
    candidates=np.arange(n)
    for epoch in range(epochs):
       if len(pts)==target: break
       # shuffle candidates
       cand = candidates[(rng.random(n)-used)>0]? Better: valid = (~used) & (not np.isin?); valid_indices = np.nonzero(~used)[0]
       order = valid_indices; rng.shuffle(order)
       added=False
       for x in order:
          if used[x]: continue
          # compute diffs = abs(np.asarray(pts) - x)
          if len(pts)>0:
             d = np.abs(pts - x)
             # if any count[d] >= max_diff -> conflict
             if np.any(counts[d] >= max_diff): continue
          # add x
          used[x]=True; pts.append(x)
          if len(pts)>0:
             for d in np.abs(pts_old - x): counts[d]+=1
          added=True
          if len(pts)==target: break
       if not added: maybe start new? break or try restart
    return pts
```

Vectorized approach:
```

valid = np.nonzero(~used)[0]
# compute all differences between valid candidates and pts? shape len(valid)*len(pts)

rng.shuffle(valid_ok)
# take first? But if we add one, need recompute. We can add multiple per epoch: pick first valid_ok[0], update, then recompute? Could update incrementally.

Computing diffs n×k ~0.67M, boolean; for each add? If recompute after each add (100 times), 67M, okay maybe 1s. But for many lengths maybe heavy. We can instead in each pass add as many as possible by scanning valid_ok and checking point remains valid? Since adding a point doesn't make previously valid candidates invalid? Actually it can: new used diffs can invalidate other candidates. If we precomputed conflicts before adding, after adding some, some candidates in valid_ok may become invalid. We can sort by random, then for each candidate, re-check conflict quickly (scalar/numpy) before add. That reduces recomputation.

- Maintain for each difference d a bitset of forbidden candidate positions? For T=1, bitset of positions that would create duplicate difference.    - For each used diff d: forbidden_mask |= bit(x+d) | bit(x-d). Because any future point at distance d from x duplicates. We check.

   - Additionally, for used diffs, if x±d is an existing point? It might be; but existing points are used and skip. Fine.

This bitset algorithm will find maximal set under T=1 fast. For T>1, need counts; forbidden when count reaches T. We can maintain counts and when adding x, for each old y, d=abs; counts[d]++; if counts[d]==T, then for all z in pts (including x) forbid z±d. Let's verify bitset update correctness for T=1:
State: pts set S; used_diff D = {|x-y|: x,y∈S}. Invariant: forbidden mask F contains all z∉S such that ∃d∈D, ∃y∈S: |z-y|=d. Then z valid iff not used and not forbidden.
When add x valid:
- New S'=S∪{x}; D'=D∪D_new where D_new={|x-y|: y∈S}.
Need update F to F' = all z∉S' with ∃d'∈D',∃y∈S': |z-y|=d'.
Start F (for S,D). Add x: used x (so ignore). For d∈D (old saturated all), need forbid x±d (z at distance d from new point x). So loop D and OR bits x±d.
For d∈D_new that was not in D, need for all y∈S' forbid y±d. Loop D_new_not_old y.
For d∈D_new already in D, already saturated and F old had forbids for old S; need add x±d, already done in old D loop if d in D. Good. For d in D_new and old? yes.
Thus correct. Complexity: maintain D as boolean/int mask? Need iterate over D list. Number D up to ~k(k-1)/2 ≤ 5000. For add x, loop over D (5000) to forbid x±d for old D; and loop new D (≤k) times pts (≤k) ~10k. For k=100 average D~2500 => 250k bit ops. Python big int OR with masks of length n bits; each OR creates big int of ~700 bytes, 250k*700 ~175MB operations, okay maybe 0.1s? Might be fine. But bit(x+d) where x+d int; we can precompute bit masks for positions? Precompute `bit[pos] = 1 << pos` for n up to maybe parent length 5582? Big ints 5582 bits; list 5582 of 700 bytes ~4MB, okay. But for many lengths, precompute per length. Or use `1 << pos` cheap? Doing 250k shifts of 5500-bit int maybe costly. Precompute bits for each n. Memory for n=5582 okay; for many lengths small. For each add: `for d in D_list: if x+d<n: forb[x+d]=True` in Python 2500 loops, 100 adds =250k, okay. New diff loops ~50k. Python 300k loops per set, trivial. This may be faster than big ints. Let's use boolean arrays.

Algorithm `make_sidon_bit(n, target_size, max_diff=1, start_points=None)`:
```
used=np.zeros(n,bool); forbidden=np.zeros(n,bool); diff_count=np.zeros(n,int8) (or bool for T1)
diff_list=[]  # list of differences with count>=T? For T1 just used diffs
pts=[]
def add_point(x, update_forbidden=True):
   if used[x] or forbidden[x] or x<0/x>=n: return False
   used[x]=True; pts.append(x)
   # For existing saturated diffs, forbid x±d
   for d in diff_list: # if T>1, diff list saturated
       if x + d < n: forbidden[x+d]=True
       if x - d >=0: forbidden[x-d]=True
   # For each old y, update diff count
   if max_diff==1:
       new_diffs=[]
       for y in pts[:-1]:
          d=abs(x-y)
          if d==0: continue
          if diff_count[d]==0:
             diff_count[d]=1
             diff_list.append(d)
             # forbid y2±d for all y2 in pts (including x)
             # But if just appended, use pts after x
             for y2 in pts:
                if y2+d<n: forbidden[y2+d]=True
                if y2-d>=0: forbidden[y2-d]=True
          else:
             # This shouldn't happen if x valid? It could if d used by pair not involving x? But x valid forbids all used diffs relative to old pts, so d not used.
             pass
   else:
       for y in pts[:-1]:
          d=abs(x-y)
          old=diff_count[d]
          diff_count[d]=old+1
          if old+1 == max_diff:
              diff_list.append(d)
              for y2 in pts:
                 forbid...
   # used pos should also be considered forbidden? We can set forbidden[x]=True to skip; used check sufficient.
   return True
```
But when adding x, we first loop diff_list to forbid x±d. This includes positions that may be old points? Bitset greedy implementation details no closures:

```
def add_sidon_point(pts, used, forbidden, diff_count, diff_list, x, n, max_diff):
    if x < 0 or x >= n or used[x] or forbidden[x]:
        return False
    used[x] = True
    pts.append(x)
    # forbid x +/- d for already saturated differences
    for d in diff_list:
        if x + d < n:
            forbidden[x + d] = True
        if x - d >= 0:
            forbidden[x - d] = True
    # update diffs with old points
    old_len = len(pts) - 1
    for idx in range(old_len):
        y = pts[idx]
        d = x - y
        if d < 0: d = -d
        if d == 0: continue
        if max_diff == 1:
            if diff_count[d] == 0:
                diff_count[d] = 1
                diff_list.append(d)
                # mark all current pts +/- d
                for z in pts:
                    if z + d < n:
                        forbidden[z + d] = True
                    if z - d >= 0:
                        forbidden[z - d] = True
        else:
            old_count = diff_count[d]
            diff_count[d] = old_count + 1
            if old_count + 1 == max_diff:
                diff_list.append(d)
                for z in pts:
                    ...
    forbidden[x] = True  # optional
    return True
```
But careful: In `for d in diff_list` before updating, if `x-d` or `x+d` includes a point that will be used later? fine. If diff_list is long (5000), loop per add heavy but okay. We can optimize by maintaining `saturated_offsets` list and boolean arrays.

For T=1, can use `diff_used` boolean and no counts. `diff_list` used diffs.

def greedy_sidon(n, max_size, max_diff=1, seed=0, start=None, max_passes=20):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    used=np.zeros(n,bool); forbidden=np.zeros(n,bool)
    diff_count = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int16) if max_diff>1 else None
    diff_list=[]
    pts=[]
    if start is not None:
       add...
    passes=0
    while len(pts)<max_size and passes<max_passes:
       # get candidate indices not used/forbidden
       cand = np.nonzero((~used) & (~forbidden))[0]
       if len(cand)==0: break
       # random shuffle or heuristic choose
       # Instead of random order, choose maybe candidate with fewest new conflicts? For simplicity random sample.
       # We can try to add multiple in one pass: iterate over shuffled cand, for each if still valid add.
       if cand.size > 200:
           # take random subset? But a valid candidate may be in subset; if none, pass continues. Better shuffle all.
           idx = cand
       else:
           idx = cand
       # shuffle
       order = idx.copy()
       rng.shuffle(order)
       added_any=False
       for x in order:
           if x<0 or x>=n or used[x] or forbidden[x]: continue
           if add_sidon_point(...):
               added_any=True
               if len(pts)==max_size: break
       passes +=1
       if not added_any: break
    return pts
```
Shuffling all cand each pass length up to 5582, passes maybe 10, okay. But after adding points, many cand invalid; still loop. Could improve by after each add, continuing with same order; some may now forbidden. Fine.

Implement `generate_golomb_greedy(n, target_marks, strategy, seed)`:
- Marks list [0], used_diff boolean length n (or up to n). last=0.

```
x_vals = np.arange(last+1, n)
diffs = x_vals[:,None] - marks[None,:]  # positive
valid = ~used_diff[diffs].any(axis=1)
valid_indices = np.nonzero(valid)[0]
```

Alternative maintain forbidden positions for future right additions: When marks set S, a candidate x>last is valid iff for all y∈S, x-y not in used_diff. Our global bitset/forbidden algorithm can add points in any order. To implement Mian-Chowla, after each add, find the smallest `x > last` with not used & not forbidden. Since forbidden includes constraints with all marks, and used. But global forbidden as defined forbids any future position at used difference from any mark. It doesn't distinguish left/right; works. So we can use a `valid_positions` boolean or sorted list. When adding, update forbidden. To find next smallest >last, we can use `np.nonzero((~used)&(~forbidden))[0]` and choose first >last. This gives Mian-Chowla if we started with 0 and always choose smallest >last. The global forbidden invariant holds for adding any point, so valid. Nice.

Thus we can implement a general greedy with **selection strategy** over valid positions:
- State used, forbidden, diff_count/diff_list (T=1 or T>1).
- Start with first mark 0 (or maybe random 0 to fix translation; if start other, we can translate at end to 0; starting 0 okay).
- At each step, compute valid = np.nonzero((~used)&(~forbidden))[0]. This O(n) per mark, 90*5582=500k, fine.
- Selection:
   - 'min': first valid > last (last = max(pts)).
   - 'max_not_too_far'? We can choose valid that minimizes span? Since we fix start 0 and may not want to jump too far? The first valid is minimal next position, tends minimal span (Mian).
   - 'random_first_start': choose a random valid from a prefix, maybe to diversify. But if choose too large, span grows. For target N, we always restrict candidates ≤N-1 (array length n) and valid. We can select based on a temperature: choose among valid positions with weight exp(-x/temperature) or first random up to e.g. last + window. Better to stay small.
   - We can also choose valid that minimizes number of new diffs? For T=1, any add creates new diffs to old marks; number new = count of distances not used. It might be k (if none used). Some positions may create distances already used? But if a distance used, forbidden would disallow (since x at used diff from some mark). So all new distances are unused; number new = k always? If x not at used diff from any mark, each d=x-y is new. Could two y produce same d? That would imply y equal, so no. So number new exactly k. So no choice.
   - For T>1, different counts matter.
- The greedy choices produce different rulers. We can run many seeds selecting random among valid positions with bias to small positions.

But array n limits; if no valid before N, stop. For random variants, we can allow span up to some S (maybe N_target + extra) then after construction translate and check span ≤N-1? If we run with array length S larger, we can generate a ruler of target size with span S, then if span ≤n-1 use. For n=5582, S maybe 6000 to give more room; then fallback if span<5582. We can generate with S=6500 and if final span<=5581, translate. This could find target size 87 not possible within n but possible within 6500; if span happens <5582. We can run multiple S/target.

We could use a heuristic from literature: The "random restart greedy" algorithm for Golomb rulers: start with k marks random, then iteratively move marks to reduce span, maintaining all differences distinct. Local search for Golomb ruler (T=1):
- No fixed k? We can start with a Golomb set from Mian-Chowla of size k with span S0 maybe >N-1.
- Objective minimize last mark (span).
- Moves: choose an index i (not 0 maybe), choose new position x in [marks[i-1]+1, marks[i+1]-1] (for sorted) maybe or any position not colliding. Try to move mark i to a new x (could be >last to reduce? no). If x = current skip. Check if all differences with other marks are distinct and not duplicate with existing differences other than pairs involving i. Efficient check:
   - Remove differences involving mark i from a dict/set (O(k)), insert new differences; if no collision, move valid.
   - For k≤100, O(k^2) per candidate, small. We can do simulated annealing: at each step, randomly choose i and candidate x (e.g., current ± random small, or random gap), evaluate if valid, accept if span decreases or with probability based on increase. But if current span >N, accept increases to escape? We can just hill-climb minimizing span; random moves may get stuck. Use tabu? Time budget enough.
- We can also **remove endpoint last and reinsert** at a smaller valid position: fix first k-1 marks, place last at smallest possible >prev that keeps all differences distinct. This is like recomputing last. If cannot reduce, remove another mark and reinsert. This is a standard repair.
   - For a given set of k-1 marks (Golomb), the minimal valid position for the kth mark >max(prev) can be found by scanning. If we remove the last mark, place it at smallest valid, span may reduce. Then remove a random mark, reinsert at smallest valid position that maintains order? But reinserting a removed mark could be placed between others, not necessarily at end; need choose position in any gap. For simplicity, we can remove last and reinsert; if no improvement, remove some other mark and reinsert at the smallest valid position overall (could be before last, but then sorted order changes and last may remain). This can compress interior.
- This local search can be implemented using a set of differences for speed. For each move, O(k^2) to recompute all differences? k small, okay.

Objective for dense offcenter: minimize `max_{k≠n-1} C_k / S^2`. Scale S=1. This is a quadratically constrained nonconvex. Need find sequences with max <0.75/n. Uniform max = (n-1)/n^2 ≈1/n (score 2). We need 25% lower. This is possible via optimizing distance distribution and boundary effects.

How to approach? Since objective is max of quadratic forms over simplex. We can use **multiplicative weight / mirror descent** with subgradient from peak off-center. Previous subgrad did smoothing but may have struggled. We can improve by:
- Explicitly exclude center from max.
- Use **joint subgradient of top peaks** with both positive/negative lags.
- Use **line search / trust region** to reduce peak.
- Use **randomized restarts**, **adaptive length** (if length free).
- Maybe use **convex relaxation**: For fixed max L, can we solve feasibility? Constraints ∑ p_i p_{i+d} ≤ L for all d. Nonconvex due to products. But for nonnegative p, can take square roots? Let p_i = x_i^2? Then ∑ x_i^2 x_j^2 ≤ L, not convex. Or use p_i positive and constraints p^T B_d p ≤ L; B_d symmetric 0-1. The set of p is not convex because B_d indefinite? B_d for distance d has offdiag ones, eigenvalues cos? Could have negative eigenvalues, so nonconvex. But maybe we can optimize using eigenvalue bounds.

Alternative parameterization: p_i as squared magnitudes of Fourier transform. Convolution is inverse FT of |F|^2. Want linear autocorr low. Let q_i be complex sequence with |q_i|^2=p_i? Then circular autocorr is inverse FT of |Q|^2. We can choose Q with flat magnitude to make circular autocorr low, then p? Hmm.

If we require p_i nonnegative, maybe choose p_i = |u_i|^2 for a length-n complex sequence u. Full linear conv of p? Not directly. But if p is the power spectrum of a sequence? Let p_k = |U_k|^2 for k=0..n-1. The time-domain autocorrelation of u has power spectrum p. We want the time-domain sequence nonnegative? We could choose u such that its autocorrelation h has low offpeak and h is nonnegative? But our p (heights) would be the power spectrum, not values. Then convolution of p is not autocorrelation of u. There is a duality: If p is a probability vector, its convolution p*p in time corresponds to |P|^2 in frequency. We want time conv low except center; so we want |P|^2 flat (low peak in time). This is like designing a sequence whose *power spectrum* is flat and whose time vector is nonnegative. Uniform p gives P a delta at 0, time conv triangular. We want p nonnegative with flat |P|^2. This is a sequence with low autocorrelation? Actually p*p in time is autocorrelation (linear) of p. So we want low *aperiodic autocorrelation* of a nonnegative sequence. This is a known problem: nonnegative aperiodic autocorrelation sequences with low peak sidelobe. There are constructions from Barker? Barker are signed. Nonnegative can use **periodic Golomb rulers?** Maybe optimal is a two-level sequence with levels determined by a low-autocorrelation signed sequence. Let p_i = (1 + α s_i)/n where s_i ∈ ±1. Then p≥0 if α≤1. The offdiag linear conv:
C_d ≈ (1-d/n)(1 + α^2 S_d)/n + boundary terms, where S_d is normalized circular autocorr of s? For d=1, need reduce (n-1)/n^2 by factor 0.75 => need α^2 S_1 negative about -0.25. But S_d = (1/n)∑_{cyclic} s_i s_{i+d}. For ±1 sequence, autocorr S_1 cannot be too negative at all d due toParseval. Alternating gives S_odd=-1, S_even=1. Need all S_d ~ -0.25? Impossible by energy as before? But linear has triangular factor and boundary terms maybe allow. Maybe choose multi-level s to achieve negative average autocorr across all d negative. The average of cyclic autocorr over d≠0 is -1/(n-1) if mean 0, very close 0, not -0.25. So cannot reduce all via two-level uniformly. But small-lag max only at d=1; we only need reduce d=1..maybe n/2? Actually max under uniform at d=1. We can focus on reducing small lags by having negative autocorr there, while allowing larger lags higher up to 0.75/n. This is easier. We can design s with strong negative short-range autocorrelation (alternating) but long-range not too high. Alternating has S_odd=-1 (great for small odd), S_even=1 (bad). If we use a **Chirp** or **M-sequence** with negative short-range? M-sequences S_d≈0, not reduce. Need a sequence with *negative low-frequency*? Actually short-range negative correlation means high-frequency variations. But all lags up to some range need negative? To keep max below 0.75/n for all d, we need reduction for all d from uniform triangular (n-d)/n^2. The required circular autocorr factor A_d must satisfy (1-d/n)(1 + α^2 A_d) ≤0.75 (1-d/n) for all d -> A_d≤-0.333 (if first-order). That's average negative for all d, impossible (mean 0). But linear boundary tail can subtract? For small d, tail small; for large d, uniform part smaller so less reduction needed. Maybe need A_d negative only for small d, close to 0 for large. The required A_d(d) = 0.75 - 1 = -0.25? independent of triangular? Wait C_d = (1-d/n) * (1/n) + (α^2/ n)(1-d/n) A_d + linear terms if s mean not zero. So ratio C_d / (1/n) ≈ (1-d/n)(1+α^2 A_d). For d where (1-d/n) small, A_d can be positive as long as (1-d/n)(1+α^2 A_d) ≤0.75. For d near n, base ≤0.75 automatically. Let's formulate a practical dense optimizer:
- Normalize S=1.
- Objective fast: compute convolution via FFT, take max excluding center index n-1 (and maybe also including center? We'll calibrate). We can also compute gradient/subgradient:
   Let C = conv(p,p). Pick a set of peak off-center indices K (e.g., top few or all above threshold). For a peak index k, the corresponding mirror vector m_i = p_{k-i} over valid range. The subgradient of C_k is mirror. The score g = (2n/S^2) C_max (scale invariant). Under constraint sum p=constant, gradient for p_i (positive) from peak k: S * mirror_i - (2 C_max? with top weighting). More generally, derivative of f = M/S^2 with M=max C_k. At peak k*: df/dp_i = (S^2 grad C_k -2M S p_i)/S^4 = (S grad C_k -2M p_i)/S^3. For mirror descent, we can update in log p: new log p += lr*(grad? ). Previous subgrad used a = a - lr*(mirror*sum_a - weighted_peak) then normalize. That's essentially gradient of M - λ S^2, not exactly. We can use mirror descent on simplex: p_i <- p_i * exp(η * (-grad_i)) then normalize. This often better.

Given objective peak, we can do **Duchi/Extragradient**:
   For current p, compute C. Identify active peaks (off-center) maybe all with C >= 0.9*M. For each active peak, mirror_i = p_{(k-i mod? for linear valid)}. Gradient component g_i = (1/|A|)∑_{k∈A} mirror_i (or weighted by C_k/M). Then update `u_i = log(p_i) - η * (g_i * S - α*p_i?)`. Actually to minimize M/S^2, scale invariance. Fix S=1, so p sum1. Then M = max C_k. Minimize M. Since sum fixed, just minimize M. Gradient of M at peak k is mirror_i. We need project back to simplex with nonneg, sum1. Mirror descent on simplex: p_i <- p_i exp(-η*g_i) / Z, where g_i is subgradient of M (since constraint sum handled by normalization). This reduces peak. Use step size adaptive.

Another angle: Since objective lower bounds average distance, we can attempt to equalize all off-center C_d to target using **multiplicative updates** per lag: For each lag d, if C_d > L, reduce products at that lag. Need implement internal FFT convolution correctly and fast. Use `numpy.fft` or `scipy.fft`. Scipy may be available. Use `numpy.fft.rfft`? For real input. Function:
```
def convolve_fft(a):
    n = len(a)
    out_len = 2*n-1
    size = 1 << (out_len-1).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, size)
    return c[:out_len]
```
Numerical errors small; max could be slightly negative at zeros; use np.maximum? For internal peak, take real.

For very small lengths, direct conv via np.convolve maybe exact? Use FFT okay.

Let p positive length n, sum=1. Internal objective J(p)=max_{k in mask} C_k, C=conv(p,p). We want minimize J. Subgradient at p for active set A (weighted) is G_i = ∑_{k∈A} w_k p[k-i] valid. Since J is max, any convex combo of peak gradients is subgradient. We can use active peaks above threshold.
Mirror descent for minimizing smooth part? On simplex, step:
`new_logp = logp - η * G`
Then renormalize p=exp(new_logp); p/=sum(p). Because sum constraint; if subtract constants, normalized same. In log-space, normalize: `new_logp -= log(sum(exp(new_logp)))` for stability. To avoid underflow, subtract max.

def dense_mirror_off(p, deadline, mask_mode, eval_every=1000, top_k=8, beta=0.9, base_lr_list=(0.05,0.03,0.08), seed=0):
    n=len(p)
    p=p.copy(); p=np.maximum(p,1e-12); p/=p.sum()
    logp=np.log(p)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_int=-1? actually score lower
    best_int=internal_score(p, mask_mode)
    best_actual=None? We'll evaluate externally maybe.
    lr=base_lr
    v=np.zeros(n); stuck=0
    while time<deadline:
       C=conv_fft(p)
       peak=C.copy(); if excl: peak[n-1] = -inf
       M=float(np.max(peak))
       # If M < target maybe stop? target internal ~ target/(2n)
       # select top peaks
       kvals = np.argpartition(peak, -top_k)[-top_k:]
       # maybe include all peaks with peak > 0.99*M but top enough
       weights = peak[kvals]; weights = np.maximum(1e-12, weights)
       # can downweight central? mask off.
       weights = weights/weights.sum()
       mirror = np.zeros(n)
       for k,w in zip(kvals,weights): ...
       g = mirror * n
       g -= g.mean()
       scale = np.max(np.abs(g))
       if scale<1e-14: perturb
       g = g/scale
       v = beta*v + (1-beta)*g
       # occasionally zero v
       logp = logp - lr*v
       # normalize logp stable
       mx = np.max(logp); logp -= mx; p = np.exp(logp); p/=p.sum()
       # store logp? if we subtract mx, p same; but logp relative lost? We can store logp = np.log(p)
       # Actually if we modify logp by subtract mx, it's fine; keep.
       logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)  # to avoid -inf? This costs. Could maintain without exact normalization?
```

- `peaks_and_mirror(C, n, top_k, mode='top')`:

- `update_mirror(p, mirror, lr, momentum, beta, mode)`:
   `g = mirror * n`
   `g -= mean(g)`
   `scale = max(abs(g))`; if scale<1e-12: return p, v, False
   `g/=scale`
   if mode in ['mirror','mirror_mom']:
      if momentum: `v = beta*v + (1-beta)*g; g=v`
      `p = p * np.exp(-lr * g)`
      `p = np.maximum(p, 1e-300); p/=p.sum()`
   if 'inverse':
      `p = 1.0/(1.0 + lr * np.clip(g, -10, 10))`? If g centered, can be negative; clip positive denominator >0. `p = 1/(1+lr*g)`; normalize.

      `p = p - lr*g; p=np.maximum(p,0); normalize`.

Let's design `optimize_sequence(a, deadline, config, actual_threshold=None)`:
Inputs: a array sum1, config dict with keys: variant, lr, beta, topk, eval_every. It returns `best_a, best_peak, best_actual` (actual maybe). It can call `get_actual` at intervals.
Pseudo:
```
def optimize_sequence(a, deadline, variant, lr, beta, topk, eval_every, seed):
    n=len(a)
    a=a.copy(); a=np.maximum(a,1e-12); a/=a.sum()
    v=np.zeros(n)
    best=a.copy(); best_peak=internal_peak(a, mask)
    best_actual = get_actual(a.tolist())
    steps=0
    stuck=0
    lr_cur=lr
    last_eval=time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 5:
       C=fft_conv(a)
       C = np.maximum(C, 0) # maybe
       if excl_center: C_excl=C.copy(); C_excl[n-1]=-1
       else: C_excl=C
       peak=float(np.max(C_excl))
       if peak < best_peak - 1e-15:
           best_peak=peak; best=a.copy(); stuck=0
       else:
           stuck += 1
       # If stuck large, perturb
       if stuck > 5000:
          a=best + 0.02*np.random.standard_normal(n); a=np.maximum(a,1e-12); a/=a.sum(); v=0; stuck=0
       # select top peaks
       idx = np.argpartition(C_excl, -topk)[-topk:]
       vals = C_excl[idx]
       # maybe smooth weights: weights = np.maximum(vals - 0.9*max,0)
       weights = vals / vals.sum()
       mirror=np.zeros(n)
       for wi,ki in zip(weights, idx):
           lo=max(0, ki-n+1); hi=min(ki,n-1)
           # mirror[lo:hi+1] += wi * a[ki - lo : ki - hi +1][::-1]? Need ensure length
           # indices = np.arange(lo,hi+1); src = a[ki - indices]
           src_start = ki - hi; src_end = ki - lo + 1
           mirror[lo:hi+1] += wi * a[src_start:src_end][::-1]
       g = mirror * n
       g -= g.mean()
       scale=np.max(np.abs(g))
       if scale < 1e-15:
           # perturb
           a += 0.01*np.random.standard_normal(n); a=np.maximum(a,1e-12); a/=a.sum(); v=0; steps+=1; continue
       g = g / scale
       if beta > 0:
           v = beta*v + (1-beta)*g
           # prevent v large
           vmax=np.max(np.abs(v)); if vmax>10: v/=vmax
           step_vec=v
       else:
           step_vec=g
       if variant == 'mirror':
           factor = np.exp(-lr_cur * step_vec)
           a = a * factor
           a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
           a /= a.sum()
       elif variant == 'inverse':
           denom = 1.0 + lr_cur * step_vec
           denom = np.maximum(denom, 1e-6)
           a = 1.0 / denom
           a /= a.sum()
       else: # subtract
           a = a - lr_cur * step_vec
           a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
           a /= a.sum()
       steps += 1
       # adaptive lr? if no improvement 1000, lr*=1.2 up to ...
       if steps % eval_every == 0:
           act = get_actual(a.tolist())
           if act < best_actual:
               best_actual=act
               # maybe update best? For actual, best should be a.copy
               # but internal best may differ; keep separate? We can maintain best_by_actual.
           # actual on best internal too occasionally
           if steps % (2*eval_every)==0:
               act_best = get_actual(best.tolist())
               if act_best < best_actual: best_actual=act_best; best=a? no
       # If time for actual maybe heavy; set eval_every based on n
    final_actual = get_actual(a.tolist()) and best_actual
    return best_a, best_peak, best_actual
```

Let's refine long dense update to be efficient and robust. We'll implement a `run_opt` function with a config.

Pseudo top-level:
```
def run_optimization(a, deadline, variant, lr, beta, topk, max_topk, actual_interval, excl_center, do_actual=True, seed=0):
    a = np.array(a, float)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12); a /= a.sum()
    n = len(a)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    v = np.zeros(n)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_peak = internal_peak(a, excl_center)
    best_actual_seq = best_a.copy()
    best_actual = get_actual(best_actual_seq.tolist()) if do_actual else -np.inf
    step=0
    stuck=0
    lr_cur=lr
    while time < deadline-1:
        C = conv(a)
        if excl_center:
            Cpk = C.copy(); Cpk[n-1] = -np.inf
        else:
            Cpk = C
        peak = float(np.max(Cpk))
        if peak < best_peak - 1e-14:
            best_peak = peak
            best_a = a.copy()
            stuck = 0
            if best_peak < 1e-15: break
        else:
            stuck += 1
        # adaptive lr
        if step % 50 == 0:
            if peak < best_peak * 0.995:
                lr_cur = min(0.5, lr_cur * 1.05)
            elif peak > best_peak * 1.05:
                lr_cur = max(0.005, lr_cur * 0.95)
        # top peaks
        if topk >= len(Cpk):
            idx = np.arange(len(Cpk))
            vals = Cpk[idx]
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(Cpk, -topk)[-topk:]
            vals = Cpk[idx]
        vals = vals - min(0, vals.min()) + 1e-12
        wsum = vals.sum()
        if wsum <=0: ...
        mirror = np.zeros(n)
        for ki, wi in zip(idx, vals/wsum):
            lo = max(0, ki - n + 1); hi = min(ki, n - 1)
            if hi >= lo:
                # slice source from ki-hi to ki-lo inclusive
                mirror[lo:hi+1] += wi * a[ki - hi:ki - lo + 1][::-1]
        g = mirror * n
        g -= g.mean()
        scale = np.max(np.abs(g))
        if scale < 1e-14:
            # random small perturb
            a = best_a + 0.01 * rng.standard_normal(n)
            a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12); a /= a.sum(); v=0; step+=1; continue
        g /= scale
        if beta > 0:
            v = beta * v + (1 - beta) * g
            vmax = np.max(np.abs(v))
            if vmax > 10: v /= vmax
            gv = v
        else:
            gv = g
        if variant == 'mirror':
            a = a * np.exp(-lr_cur * gv)
            a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
            a /= a.sum()
        elif variant == 'inverse':
            denom = 1.0 + lr_cur * gv
            denom = np.maximum(denom, 1e-6)
            a = 1.0 / denom
            a /= a.sum()
        else:
            a = a - lr_cur * gv
            a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
            a /= a.sum()
        step += 1
        if do_actual and step % actual_interval == 0:
            act = get_actual(a.tolist())
            if act < best_actual:
                best_actual=act; best_actual_seq=a.copy()
            # also eval best_a occasionally
        if stuck > 8000:
            a = best_a + 0.02 * rng.standard_normal(n)
            a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12); a /= a.sum(); v=0
            stuck=0
            # maybe change lr
    if do_actual:
        act_curr=get_actual(a.tolist())
        if act_curr < best_actual: best_actual=act_curr; best_actual_seq=a.copy()
        act_best=get_actual(best_a.tolist())
        if act_best < best_actual: best_actual=act_best; best_actual_seq=best_a.copy()
    return best_actual_seq if do_actual else best_a, best_actual if do_actual else best_peak
```
Need `max_topk` not used. For small n, topk can be full.

Calibration implementation:
```
def calibrate_objective(n=128, seed=0):
    vectors=[]
    vectors.append(np.full(n,1.0/n))
    # one endpoint high 0.5 rest uniform
    v2=np.full(n,1.0/n); v2[0]+=0.5; v2 /= v2.sum()
    vectors.append(v2)
    # alternation
    v3=np.full(n,1.0/n); v3[::2]+=0.3/n; v3[1::2]-=0.3/n; v3=np.maximum(v3,1e-6); v3/=v3.sum()
    # random
    v4=np.random.default_rng(1).dirichlet(np.ones(n));
    # sparse 5
    idx=...; v5 zeros, v5[idx]=1/5
    actuals=[]
    for v: actuals.append(get_actual(v.tolist()))
    incl=[]; excl=[]
    for v:
       C=conv(v); incl.append(2*n*np.max(C)); if n>1: Cex=C.copy(); Cex[n-1]=-inf; excl.append(2*n*np.max(Cex))
    # choose lower log error if finite
```
But if actual internally differs by scale (e.g., target 1.5), errors ratio. We'll just choose if `sum_err_excl < sum_err_incl - 1e-6`. If tie, incl.

However, if hidden objective all (prompt), actual for endpoint high maybe much larger internal inclusive; both computed exactly, so incl error 0, excl error large. Good.

Now, we need ensure code does not use lambdas/closures. All helper top-level. No filesystem/network.

Let's detail constructors:
```
def make_uniform(n): return np.full(n,1.0/n)
def make_endpoint_lift(n, lift=10.0, width=None):
   if width is None: width=max(2,n//100)
   w=np.ones(n)
   w[:width]+=lift
   w[-width:]+=lift
   if n <= 2*width: maybe overlap; okay.
   w=np.maximum(w,1e-9); return w/w.sum()
def make_endpoint_alt(n,lift=5,width=None):
   parity = np.ones(n); parity[::2]=1; parity[1::2]=lift? Maybe base+lift*parity? Use lift=5 for even, low for odd.
   w=np.ones(n); w[:width]+=lift*(1+parity[:width]); ...
```
But parity high/low in endpoint regions maybe. Simpler:
```
w=np.ones(n); mask=np.zeros(n); mask[:width]=1; mask[-width:]=1; alt=np.ones(n); alt[::2]=1; alt[1::2]=0; w = 1 + lift*mask*alt
```

def small_search(deadline, excl, best_seq, best_score):
    lengths=[2,3,4,5,6,7,8,9,10,12,16,20,24,32,40,48,64]
    configs=[('mirror',0.1,0.9,5), ('mirror',0.05,0.95,8), ('inverse',0.1,0.0,5)]
    for L in lengths:
       if time > deadline - 20: break
       # direct starts: uniform, endpoint lift width 2, alternating, random
       starts=[uniform(L), endpoint_lift(L,2,width=max(1,L//4)), cosine(L,1,0.5,0), random smooth]
       for cfg in configs:
          for s in starts:
             if time > deadline - 20: break
             slice_deadline = min(deadline, time+max(5, 30/len(lengths)?))
             res = run_optimization(s, slice_deadline, cfg, do_actual=True, excl_center=excl, actual_interval=1000, seed=...)
             best_seq,best_score = consider(res, ...)
             if best_score < 1.05: return
```

def consider_array(a, best_seq, best_score, label):
    try:
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
        if a.size==0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return best_seq,best_score
        a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
        if a.sum()<0.01: a = np.maximum(a, 1e-6); a = np.minimum(a,1000)

`get_actual`:
```
def get_actual(seq):
    try:
        vals=[float(x) for x in seq]
        return float(evaluate_sequence(vals))
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')
```
Assume evaluate_sequence in global.

`convolve`:
```
def do_convolve(a):
    n=len(a)
    if n < 256:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    out=n*2-1
    size=1 << (out-1).bit_length()
    fa = rfft(a, size)
    c = irfft(fa*fa, size)
    return c[:out]
```
Use scipy.fft. If scipy not available? Prompt allows. Fallback to np.fft? Could just use np.fft to reduce dependency? SciPy allowed. `np.fft.rfft` okay. Implement:
```
try: from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft
except: from numpy.fft import rfft, irfft
```
Top-level import in try okay.

`internal_peak(a, excl)`:
```
def internal_peak(a, excl=False):
    S = float(np.sum(a))
    if S <= 1e-15: return np.inf
    p = a/S
    C=do_convolve(p)
    if excl and len(C)>1:
        Cpk = C.copy(); Cpk[len(p)-1] = -np.inf
        return float(np.max(Cpk))
    return float(np.max(C))
```
For run_opt, we compute C each iter to avoid double.

Constructors top-level:
```
def make_uniform(n): return np.full(n,1.0/n)

def make_perturbed(a, sigma, seed):
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   x=a + sigma*rng.standard_normal(a.size)
   x=np.maximum(x,1e-12); return x/np.sum(x)

def make_endpoint_lift(n, lift=10.0, width=None): ...

Now, calibration `calibrate_exclusive` should not be too slow. Use `get_actual` for test vectors. If actual is universal? Let's implement:
```
def calibrate_exclusive(n=128, seed=0):
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    vecs=[]
    vecs.append(make_uniform(n))
    x=make_uniform(n); x[0]+=0.5; x/=x.sum(); vecs.append(x)
    y=make_endpoint_lift(n, lift=20, width=max(2,n//20)); vecs.append(y)
    z=make_ar(n, rho=-0.7, seed=1); vecs.append(z)
    s=make_random_smooth(n, seed=2); vecs.append(s)
    err_incl=0; err_excl=0; any=False
    for v in vecs:
       act=get_actual(v.tolist())
       if not np.isfinite(act) or act<=0: continue
       C=do_convolve(v/v.sum())
       incl=float(np.max(C))*2*n
       Ce=C.copy(); Ce[n-1]=-np.inf; excl=float(np.max(Ce))*2*n
       if incl>0 and excl>0:
          err_incl += abs(np.log(act/incl))
          err_excl += abs(np.log(act/excl))
          any=True
    if not any: return False
    return err_excl < err_incl - 1e-6
```
If hidden objective uses no n factor, both incl/excl off by constant; relative error? actual/incl not constant maybe because hidden factor constant vs 2n; but both internal have 2n; ratio constant, error_logs? If actual = c*peak, internal =2n*peak. For all vectors, ratio c/(2n) constant for both incl/excl? Excl ratio different because peaks differ; log errors maybe similar. The difference may still indicate mask if peaks differ. If c differs but mask same, errors similar; choose excl only if significantly lower. Good.

In long phase, if `excl` true, internal dense may ignore center; but actual best maybe short. Fine.

def propose_candidate():
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start + max(60.0, 2400.0 - 45.0)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(0)
    # parent
    parent_arr=None; n_parent=None
    try:
       h=height_sequence_1
       arr=np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
       if arr.ndim !=1 or arr.size<1: raise ValueError
       arr=np.clip(arr,0,1000)
       if np.isfinite(arr).all() and arr.sum()>0:
          parent_arr=arr/arr.sum()
          n_parent=arr.size
    except Exception:
       pass
    if n_parent is None: n_parent=5582
    # choose long n
    if 100 <= n_parent <= 20000:
       n_long = int(n_parent)
    else:
       n_long = 5582
    excl = False
    if deadline - time > 30:
       excl = calibrate_exclusive(128, 0)
       print("excl_center:", excl)
    best_seq = None; best_score = np.inf
    # consider parent if available
    if parent_arr is not None:
       best_seq,best_score = consider_array(parent_arr, best_seq,best_score,"parent")
    # short deterministic
    lengths=[2,3,4,5,6,7,8,10,12,16,20]
    for L in lengths:
       if time>deadline-20: break
       best_seq,best_score=consider_array(make_uniform(L),... f"uniform{L}")
    # optimal n=2 explicit
    best_seq,best_score=consider_array(np.array([1/3,2/3]),...,"two")
    # structures for n_long and maybe 2048
    for N in (n_long, 2048, 512): ... endpoint, ar, cosine; consider
```

Now, potential bug: `run_optimization` uses `len(Cpk)` and `topk`; for n small and topk maybe > len. Implement.

Let's write run_optimization carefully with no syntax errors.

```
def run_optimization(a, deadline, variant, base_lr, beta, topk, excl, do_actual, actual_interval, seed):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).copy()
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 0: a = np.full(a.size, 1.0/a.size); s=a.size
    a = a / s
    n = a.size
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    v = np.zeros(n)
    best_a = a.copy()
    C = do_convolve(a)
    best_peak = peak_from_C(C, excl)
    if do_actual:
       best_actual = get_actual(best_a.tolist())
       best_actual_seq = best_a.copy()
    else:
       best_actual = np.inf
       best_actual_seq = best_a.copy()
    step=0; stuck=0; lr=base_lr
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        C = do_convolve(a)
        peak = peak_from_C(C, excl)
        if not np.isfinite(peak):
            a=make_perturbed(best_a,0.02,seed=int(step)); v=0; step+=1; continue
        if peak < best_peak - 1e-15:
            best_peak=peak; best_a=a.copy(); stuck=0
        else:
            stuck += 1
        if step % 100 == 0:
            if peak < best_peak * 0.999:
                lr = min(0.4, lr*1.05)
            elif peak > best_peak * 1.02:
                lr = max(0.005, lr*0.9)
        # choose peaks
        Cpk = C.copy()
        if excl:
            Cpk[n-1] = -np.inf
        # add tiny random for tie
        Cpk = Cpk + 1e-14 * rng.random(Cpk.size)  # but -inf + noise? -inf+finite=-inf ok. If all -inf? n=1; avoid.
        eff_topk = topk
        if eff_topk > Cpk.size: eff_topk = Cpk.size
        if eff_topk < 1: eff_topk=1
        if excl and Cpk.size > 1:
            # if topk small, argpartition with -inf okay
        idx = np.argpartition(Cpk, -eff_topk)[-eff_topk:]
        vals = Cpk[idx]
        vals = vals - np.min(vals) + 1e-15
        ws = vals.sum()
        if ws <= 0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
            # perturb
            a = best_a + 0.01*rng.standard_normal(n); a=np.maximum(a,1e-12); a/=a.sum(); v=0; step+=1; continue
        mirror = np.zeros(n)
        for ki, wi in zip(idx, vals/ws):
            ki=int(ki); wi=float(wi)
            lo = ki - n + 1
            if lo < 0: lo = 0
            hi = ki
            if hi > n - 1: hi = n - 1
            if hi >= lo:
                # source indices ki-lo down to ki-hi
                start = ki - hi
                stop = ki - lo + 1
                mirror[lo:hi+1] += wi * a[start:stop][::-1]
        g = mirror * float(n)
        g -= float(np.mean(g))
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
        if scale < 1e-15 or not np.isfinite(scale):
            a = best_a + 0.02*rng.standard_normal(n)
            a = np.maximum(a,1e-12); a /= a.sum(); v=0
            step +=1
            if do_actual and step % actual_interval ==0: ...
            continue
        g = g / scale
        if beta > 0:
            v = beta * v + (1 - beta) * g
            vmax = float(np.max(np.abs(v)))
            if vmax > 10:
                v = v / vmax
                # Note: v changed but g? use v as step
            step_vec = v
        else:
            step_vec = g
        if variant == 'mirror':
            a = a * np.exp(-lr * step_vec)
            a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
            a = a / float(np.sum(a))
        elif variant == 'inverse':
            denom = 1.0 + lr * step_vec
            denom = np.maximum(denom, 1e-6)
            a = 1.0 / denom
            a = a / float(np.sum(a))
        else:
            a = a - lr * step_vec
            a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
            a = a / float(np.sum(a))
        step += 1
        if do_actual and step % actual_interval == 0:
            act = get_actual(a.tolist())
            if np.isfinite(act) and act < best_actual:
                best_actual = act
                best_actual_seq = a.copy()
        if stuck > 10000:
            a = best_a + 0.02*rng.standard_normal(n)
            a = np.maximum(a,1e-12); a /= a.sum(); v=0; lr=max(base_lr*0.5,0.01)
            stuck=0
    if do_actual:
        act_curr = get_actual(a.tolist())
        if np.isfinite(act_curr) and act_curr < best_actual:
            best_actual = act_curr; best_actual_seq = a.copy()
        act_best = get_actual(best_a.tolist())
        if np.isfinite(act_best) and act_best < best_actual:
            best_actual = act_best; best_actual_seq = best_a.copy()
        return best_actual_seq, best_actual
    return best_a, best_peak
```

def peak_from_C(C, excl):

    if excl and len(C)>1:
        C2=C.copy(); C2[n-1]=-np.inf
        return float(np.max(C2))
    return float(np.max(C))

def make_perturbed_uniform(n, rel=0.2, seed=0):
   mean=1.0/n

def propose_candidate():
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(60.0, 2400.0 - 45.0)

```python
"""
Searches for a non-negative sequence minimizing evaluate_sequence.

The program combines several ideas:
1. Fast direct/sparse evaluation of candidate sequences.
2. A small-length multi-start mirror/subgradient search, because the objective
   is scale-invariant and short sequences can have low peak self-convolution.
3. A long-length optimization from the supplied parent and structured seeds
   using mirror descent on the max coefficient of the self-convolution.
4. Light calibration to allow an alternative objective that ignores the
   middle coefficient, while still always keeping the best sequence by the
   actual evaluate_sequence() score.

All helper functions are top-level and no closures/lambda functions are used.
"""

import time
import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft
except Exception:
    from numpy.fft import rfft, irfft


def get_actual_score(seq):
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in seq]))
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def do_convolve(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
    n = len(a)
    out_len = 2 * n - 1
    if n < 256:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    size = 1 << max(0, (out_len - 1).bit_length())
    fa = rfft(a, size)
    c = irfft(fa * fa, size)
    return np.asarray(c[:out_len], dtype=float)


def peak_from_conv(c, exclude_center):
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=float)
    n = (len(c) + 1) // 2
    if exclude_center and len(c) > 1:
        c2 = c.copy()
        c2[n - 1] = -np.inf
        return float(np.max(c2))
    return float(np.max(c))


def make_uniform(n):
    n = max(1, int(n))
    return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=float)


def make_two_point(n=2, x=1.0 / 3.0):
    return np.array([float(x), float(1.0 - x)], dtype=float)


def make_perturbed(a, rel=0.2, seed=0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=float)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    scale = rel * max(float(np.mean(np.maximum(a, 0.0))),
                      0.1 * float(np.max(a)),
                      1e-12)
    b = a + scale * rng.standard_normal(a.size)
    b = np.maximum(b, 1e-12)
    b = b / float(np.sum(b))
    return b


def make_random_sequence(n, seed=0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    return rng.dirichlet(np.ones(max(1, int(n))))


def make_endpoint_lift(n, lift=10.0, width=None):
    n = max(2, int(n))
    if width is None:
        width = max(2, int(n // 100))
    width = max(1, min(width, n))
    w = np.ones(n, dtype=float)
    w[:width] += float(lift)
    w[-width:] += float(lift)
    w = np.maximum(w, 1e-12)
    return w / float(np.sum(w))


def make_endpoint_alt(n, lift=5.0, width=None):
    n = max(2, int(n))
    if width is None:
        width = max(2, int(n // 80))
    width = max(1, min(width, n))
    w = np.ones(n, dtype=float)
    mask = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    mask[:width] = 1.0
    mask[-width:] = 1.0
    alt = np.ones(n, dtype=float)
    alt[1::2] = 0.2
    w = 1.0 + float(lift) * mask * alt
    w = np.maximum(w, 1e-12)
    return w / float(np.sum(w))


def make_cosine(n, freq=1, amp=0.7, phase=0.0):
    n = max(2, int(n))
    t = np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    w = 1.0 + float(amp) * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * float(freq) * t / n + float(phase))
    w = np.maximum(w, 1e-8)
    return w / float(np.sum(w))


def make_ar(n, rho=-0.7, seed=0):
    n = max(2, int(n))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    z = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    z[0] = rng.standard_normal()
    for i in range(1, n):
        z[i] = float(rho) * z[i - 1] + rng.standard_normal()
    z = z - float(np.mean(z))
    p = np.exp(0.5 * z)
    return p / float(np.sum(p))


def make_random_smooth(n, seed=0):
    n = max(2, int(n))
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    freqs = np.arange(1, 21, dtype=float)
    amps = rng.standard_normal(freqs.size) / np.power(freqs, 1.25)
    t = np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    z = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    for f, a in zip(freqs, amps):
        z += float(a) * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * float(f) * t / n)
    p = np.exp(0.5 * z)
    return p / float(np.sum(p))


def run_optimization(a, deadline, variant, base_lr, beta, topk, exclude_center,
                     do_actual, actual_interval, seed):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        a = np.array([1.0], dtype=float)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 1e-15:
        a = make_uniform(a.size)
        s = float(a.size)
    a = a / s
    n = int(a.size)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)

    c0 = do_convolve(a)
    best_peak = peak_from_conv(c0, exclude_center)
    best_a = a.copy()

    if do_actual:
        best_actual = get_actual_score(best_a.tolist())
        best_actual_seq = best_a.copy()
    else:
        best_actual = float("inf")
        best_actual_seq = best_a.copy()

    tie_bias = (1e-18 / max(1, 2 * n - 1)) * np.arange(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    step = 0
    stuck = 0
    lr = float(base_lr)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 1.0:
        c = do_convolve(a)
        peak = peak_from_conv(c, exclude_center)
        if not np.isfinite(peak):
            a = make_perturbed(best_a, 0.05, seed=int(step & 0xffff))
            v.fill(0.0)
            step += 1
            continue

        if peak < best_peak - 1e-15:
            best_peak = peak
            best_a = a.copy()
            stuck = 0
        else:
            stuck += 1

        if step % 80 == 0:
            if peak < best_peak * 0.999:
                lr = min(0.45, lr * 1.05)
            elif peak > best_peak * 1.03:
                lr = max(0.005, lr * 0.9)

        cp = c.copy()
        if exclude_center:
            cp[n - 1] = -np.inf
        cp = cp + tie_bias

        size = int(cp.size)
        eff_topk = int(min(int(topk), max(2, size // 2)))
        eff_topk = max(1, min(eff_topk, size))
        idx = np.argpartition(cp, -eff_topk)[-eff_topk:]
        vals = cp[idx]
        vals = vals - float(np.min(vals)) + 1e-15
        wsum = float(np.sum(vals))
        if not np.isfinite(wsum) or wsum <= 0.0:
            a = best_a + 0.02 * rng.standard_normal(n)
            a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
            a /= float(np.sum(a))
            v.fill(0.0)
            step += 1
            continue

        mirror = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
        for ki, wi in zip(idx, vals / wsum):
            ki = int(ki)
            wi = float(wi)
            lo = ki - n + 1
            if lo < 0:
                lo = 0
            hi = ki
            if hi > n - 1:
                hi = n - 1
            if hi >= lo:
                st = ki - hi
                en = ki - lo + 1
                mirror[lo:hi + 1] += wi * a[st:en][::-1]

        g = mirror * float(n)
        g -= float(np.mean(g))
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
        if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale < 1e-15:
            a = best_a + 0.02 * rng.standard_normal(n)
            a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
            a /= float(np.sum(a))
            v.fill(0.0)
            step += 1
            continue

        g /= scale
        if beta > 0.0:
            v = float(beta) * v + (1.0 - float(beta)) * g
            vmax = float(np.max(np.abs(v)))
            if vmax > 10.0:
                v /= vmax
            step_vec = v
        else:
            step_vec = g

        if variant == "mirror":
            a = a * np.exp(-lr * step_vec)
            a = np.maximum(a, 1e-300)
            a /= float(np.sum(a))
        elif variant == "inverse":
            denom = 1.0 + lr * step_vec
            denom = np.maximum(denom, 1e-6)
            a = 1.0 / denom
            a /= float(np.sum(a))
        else:
            a = a - lr * step_vec
            a = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
            a /= float(np.sum(a))

        step += 1

        if do_actual and actual_interval > 0 and step % actual_interval == 0:
            act = get_actual_score(a.tolist())
            if np.isfinite(act) and act < best_actual:
                best_actual = act
                best_actual_seq = a.copy()

        if stuck > 12000:
            a = make_perturbed(best_a, 0.04, seed=int((step + 101) & 0xffff))
            v.fill(0.0)
            lr = max(0.01, base_lr * 0.6)
            stuck = 0

    if do_actual and time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.2:
        act_cur = get_actual_score(a.tolist())
        if np.isfinite(act_cur) and act_cur < best_actual:
            best_actual = act_cur
            best_actual_seq = a.copy()
        act_best = get_actual_score(best_a.tolist())
        if np.isfinite(act_best) and act_best < best_actual:
            best_actual = act_best
            best_actual_seq = best_a.copy()
        return best_actual_seq, best_actual

    return best_a, best_peak


def consider_array(a, best_seq, best_score, label):
    try:
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float)
        if a.ndim != 1 or a.size == 0:
            return best_seq, best_score
        a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(np.sum(a))
        if s <= 1e-12:
            return best_seq, best_score
        a = a / s
        sc = get_actual_score(a.tolist())
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_seq = a.copy()
            best_score = float(sc)
        print(f"consider {label}: best={best_score:.9f} len={len(a)}")
    except Exception:
        pass
    return best_seq, best_score


def calibrate_exclusive(n=96, seed=0):
    tries = [
        make_uniform(n),
        make_endpoint_lift(n, lift=10.0, width=max(2, n // 24)),
        make_endpoint_alt(n, lift=6.0, width=max(2, n // 20)),
        make_ar(n, rho=-0.7, seed=1),
        make_random_smooth(n, seed=2),
    ]
    err_inc = 0.0
    err_exc = 0.0
    cnt = 0
    for v in tries:
        act = get_actual_score(v.tolist())
        if not np.isfinite(act) or act <= 0.0:
            continue
        v = v / float(np.sum(v))
        c = do_convolve(v)
        inc = 2.0 * n * peak_from_conv(c, False)
        exc = 2.0 * n * peak_from_conv(c, True)
        if inc > 0.0 and exc > 0.0:
            err_inc += abs(np.log(act / inc))
            err_exc += abs(np.log(act / exc))
            cnt += 1
    if cnt == 0:
        return False
    return err_exc < err_inc - 1e-7


def propose_candidate():
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(60.0, 2400.0 - 45.0)

    parent = None
    n_parent = None
    try:
        parr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=float)
        if parr.ndim == 1 and parr.size >= 1:
            parr = np.where(np.isfinite(parr), parr, 0.0)
            parr = np.clip(parr, 0.0, 1000.0)
            ss = float(np.sum(parr))
            if ss > 1e-12:
                parent = parr / ss
                n_parent = int(parr.size)
    except Exception:
        parent = None
        n_parent = None

    if n_parent is None:
        n_parent = 5582
    if 100 <= n_parent <= 20000:
        long_n = int(n_parent)
    else:
        long_n = 5582

    exclude_center = False
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
        try:
            exclude_center = calibrate_exclusive(96, 17)
        except Exception:
            exclude_center = False
        print(f"calibration: exclude_center={exclude_center}")

    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    if parent is not None:
        best_seq, best_score = consider_array(parent, best_seq, best_score, "parent")

    # Direct candidate pool.
    for L in (2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10, 12, 16, 20, 32, 64, 128,
              256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30.0:
            break
        best_seq, best_score = consider_array(make_uniform(L),
                                              best_seq, best_score,
                                              f"uniform_len{L}")

    best_seq, best_score = consider_array(make_two_point(2, 1.0 / 3.0),
                                          best_seq, best_score, "len2_two_point")

    struct_ns = []
    for nn in (long_n, 512, 1024, 2048):
        if nn >= 2 and nn not in struct_ns:
            struct_ns.append(nn)

    for N in struct_ns:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30.0:
            break
        best_seq, best_score = consider_array(
            make_endpoint_lift(N, 8.0, max(4, N // 100)),
            best_seq, best_score, f"endpoint_len{N}")
        best_seq, best_score = consider_array(
            make_endpoint_alt(N, 5.0, max(4, N // 80)),
            best_seq, best_score, f"endpoint_alt_len{N}")
        best_seq, best_score = consider_array(
            make_cosine(N, 1, 0.75, 0.0),
            best_seq, best_score, f"cos_len{N}")
        best_seq, best_score = consider_array(
            make_ar(N, -0.7, 77),
            best_seq, best_score, f"ar_len{N}")

    # Small-length optimization. Short sequences can be very competitive.
    small_lengths = [2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10, 12, 16, 20, 24, 32, 40, 48, 64, 96, 128]
    small_configs = [
        ("mirror", 0.10, 0.90, 2),
        ("mirror", 0.05, 0.96, 4),
        ("mirror", 0.18, 0.80, 2),
        ("inverse", 0.10, 0.0, 3),
    ]

    small_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 260.0)
    if time.monotonic() < small_deadline:
        per_job = max(0.7, (small_deadline - time.monotonic()) /
                      max(1, len(small_lengths) * 2 * 3))
        seed = 101
        for L in small_lengths:
            if time.monotonic() > small_deadline - 5.0:
                break
            starts = [
                make_perturbed(make_uniform(L), 0.25, seed),
                make_endpoint_alt(L, 6.0, max(1, L // 5)),
                make_random_sequence(L, seed + 1),
            ]
            for cfg in small_configs[:2]:
                for st in starts:
                    if time.monotonic() > small_deadline - 5.0:
                        break
                    slice_dead = min(small_deadline, time.monotonic() + per_job)
                    res, sc = run_optimization(st, slice_dead, cfg[0], cfg[1], cfg[2],
                                               cfg[3], exclude_center, True, 400, seed)
                    best_seq, best_score = consider_array(res, best_seq, best_score,
                                                          f"small_n{L}_{cfg[0]}")
                    seed += 7

    # Focused refinement around the currently best short/medium sequence.
    if (best_seq is not None and best_score < 1.65 and
            2 <= len(best_seq) <= 1024 and
            time.monotonic() < deadline - 150.0):
        nf = int(len(best_seq))
        focus_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + min(260.0,
                                                        (deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.45))
        focus_configs = small_configs[:3]
        starts = [
            make_perturbed(best_seq, 0.10, 401),
            make_endpoint_alt(nf, 6.0, max(1, nf // 5)),
            make_random_smooth(nf, 402),
            make_perturbed(make_uniform(nf), 0.25, 403),
        ]
        per_job = max(1.0, (focus_deadline - time.monotonic()) /
                      max(1, len(focus_configs) * len(starts)))
        seed = 500
        for cfg in focus_configs:
            for st in starts:
                if time.monotonic() > focus_deadline - 5.0:
                    break
                slice_dead = min(focus_deadline, time.monotonic() + per_job)
                res, sc = run_optimization(st, slice_dead, cfg[0], cfg[1], cfg[2],
                                           cfg[3], exclude_center, True, 400, seed)
                best_seq, best_score = consider_array(res, best_seq, best_score,
                                                      f"focus_n{nf}_{cfg[0]}")
                seed += 11

    # Long-length optimization, used especially when the parent is long or
    # the best score is still not very low.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 220.0 and (best_score > 1.05 or best_seq is None):
        long_n = min(max(long_n, 32), 12000)
        inits = []
        if parent is not None and int(parent.size) == long_n:
            inits.append(parent.copy())
        inits.append(make_perturbed(make_uniform(long_n), 0.20, 601))
        inits.append(make_endpoint_lift(long_n, 10.0, max(10, long_n // 100)))
        inits.append(make_endpoint_alt(long_n, 6.0, max(10, long_n // 80)))
        inits.append(make_cosine(long_n, 1, 0.8, 0.0))
        inits.append(make_ar(long_n, -0.7, 602))

        long_configs = [
            ("mirror", 0.06, 0.90, 3),
            ("mirror", 0.03, 0.95, 6),
            ("mirror", 0.10, 0.85, 3),
            ("inverse", 0.05, 0.0, 4),
        ]
        chosen_configs = long_configs[:3]
        chosen_inits = inits[:4]

        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic() - 70.0
        jobs = max(1, len(chosen_configs) * len(chosen_inits))
        per_job = max(6.0, remaining / jobs)
        per_job = min(per_job, 220.0)

        actual_interval = 20000
        if long_n <= 256:
            actual_interval = 500
        elif long_n <= 1024:
            actual_interval = 2000
        elif long_n <= 5000:
            actual_interval = 6000
        elif long_n <= 10000:
            actual_interval = 12000

        seed = 700
        for cfg in chosen_configs:
            for st in chosen_inits:
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - 70.0:
                    break
                slice_dead = min(deadline - 65.0, time.monotonic() + per_job)
                res, sc = run_optimization(st, slice_dead, cfg[0], cfg[1], cfg[2],
                                           cfg[3], exclude_center, True,
                                           actual_interval, seed)
                best_seq, best_score = consider_array(res, best_seq, best_score,
                                                      f"long_n{long_n}_{cfg[0]}")
                seed += 13
                if best_score < 1.05:
                    break

    # Final gentle refinement from the best sequence found so far.
    if best_seq is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 15.0:
        fin = best_seq.copy()
        nn = len(fin)
        actual_interval = max(100, nn // 2)
        res, sc = run_optimization(fin, deadline - 6.0, "mirror",
                                   0.02, 0.95, 8, exclude_center,
                                   True, actual_interval, 999)
        best_seq, best_score = consider_array(res, best_seq, best_score, "final_refine")

    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = make_two_point(2, 1.0 / 3.0)

    best_seq = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=float)
    best_seq = np.where(np.isfinite(best_seq), best_seq, 0.0)
    best_seq = np.clip(best_seq, 0.0, 1000.0)
    ss = float(np.sum(best_seq))
    if ss < 0.01:
        best_seq = np.maximum(best_seq, 1e-6)
        ss = float(np.sum(best_seq))
    if ss > 0.0:
        best_seq = best_seq / ss

    final = [float(x) for x in best_seq]
    print(f"FINAL score={get_actual_score(final):.9f} len={len(final)}")
    return final
```
